                                                  \\\n        if (err_ != cudaSuccess) {                                                         \\\n            printf(\"CUDA error %s at %s:%d: %s\\n\", cudaGetErrorName(err_), __FILE__,       \\\n                   __LINE__, cudaGetErrorString(err_));                                    \\\n            exit(1);                                                                       \\\n        }                                                                                  \\\n    } while (0)\n\n// 3D index calculation\ninline constexpr size_t idx3(const size_t x, const size_t y, const size_t z, const size_t nx, const size_t ny) noexcept {\n    return z * (nx * ny) + y * nx + x;\n}\n\nvoid initializeGrid(std::vector<Real>& grid, const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const size_t nz) {\n    for (size_t z = 0; z < nz; ++z) {\n        for (size_t y = 0; y < ny; ++y) {\n            for (size_t x = 0; x < nx; ++x) {\n                const size_t idx = idx3(x, y, z, nx, ny);\n                grid[idx] = (idx % 19) * 1.0;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// CUDA 7-point stencil\n//\n// Each thread block handles a TX x TY column of the interior and marches along\n// z, keeping the z-1 / z+1 values in registers (2.5D blocking) while the\n// current xy-plane (plus its x/y halo) lives in shared memory.  Boundary cells\n// are never written: they are copied unchanged by the original algorithm on\n// every iteration, so both device buffers are seeded with the initial grid and\n// their boundaries stay valid for the whole run.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nstatic constexpr int TX = 32;\nstatic constexpr int TY = 8;\n\n__global__ __launch_bounds__(TX* TY) void stencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input,\n                                                        Real* __restrict__ output,\n                                                        const int nx, const int ny, const int nz,\n                                                        const int zChunk) {\n    __shared__ Real tile[TY + 2][TX + 2];\n\n    const int tx = threadIdx.x;\n    const int ty = threadIdx.y;\n\n    const int x = 1 + blockIdx.x * TX + tx;\n    const int y = 1 + blockIdx.y * TY + ty;\n\n    // Clamped coordinates keep the (unused) shared-memory entries of threads\n    // that fall outside the grid at valid addresses.\n    const int xl = min(x, nx - 1);\n    const int yl = min(y, ny - 1);\n\n    const bool active = (x < nx - 1) && (y < ny - 1);\n\n    const size_t slice = (size_t)nx * (size_t)ny;\n    const size_t base = (size_t)yl * (size_t)nx + (size_t)xl;\n\n    const int z0 = 1 + blockIdx.z * zChunk;\n    const int z1 = min(z0 + zChunk, nz - 1);\n    if (z0 >= z1) return;\n\n    const Real* in = input + base + (size_t)z0 * slice;\n\n    Real prev = in[-(ptrdiff_t)slice];\n    Real cur = in[0];\n\n    for (int z = z0; z < z1; ++z, in += slice) {\n        __syncthreads();\n        tile[ty + 1][tx + 1] = cur;\n        if (tx == 0) {\n            tile[ty + 1][0] = in[-1];\n        }\n        if (tx == TX - 1) {\n            tile[ty + 1][TX + 1] = in[min(x + 1, nx - 1) - xl];\n        }\n        if (ty == 0) {\n            tile[0][tx + 1] = in[-nx];\n        }\n        if (ty == TY - 1) {\n            tile[TY + 1][tx + 1] = in[(size_t)(min(y + 1, ny - 1) - yl) * nx];\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n\n        const Real next = in[slice];\n\n        if (active) {\n            const Real center = cur;\n            const Real left = tile[ty + 1][tx];\n            const Real right = tile[ty + 1][tx + 2];\n            const Real front = tile[ty][tx + 1];\n            const Real back = tile[ty + 2][tx + 1];\n            const Real bottom = prev;\n            const Real top = next;\n\n            output[(size_t)z * slice + base] =\n                (center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top) / 7.0;\n        }\n\n        prev = cur;\n        cur = next;\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<Real>& grid, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Simple sanity checks\n\n    // 1. No NaN or Inf values\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        if (std::isnan(val) || std::isinf(val)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // 2. Values should be reasonable (bounded)\n    Real minVal = grid[0];\n    Real maxVal = grid[0];\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Value range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n\n    // After averaging, values should be somewhat bounded\n    if (maxVal > 1e6 || minVal < -1e6) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // 3. both are seeded with the initial data so\n    // that the boundary cells - which the stencil only ever copies - are already\n    // in place in either buffer.\n    Real* d_grid1 = nullptr;\n    Real* d_grid2 = nullptr;\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid1, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid2, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(d_grid1, grid1.data(), gridSize * sizeof(Real), cudaMemcpyHostToDevice));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(d_grid2, grid1.data(), gridSize * sizeof(Real), cudaMemcpyHostToDevice));\n\n    const int inx = (int)nx, iny = (int)ny, inz = (int)nz;\n    const int nxi = inx - 2, nyi = iny - 2, nzi = inz - 2; iter < iterations; ++iter) {\n            const Real* in = (iter % 2 == 0) ? d_grid1 : d_grid2;\n            Real* out = (iter % 2 == 0) ? d_grid2 : d_grid1;\n            stencilKernel<<<grid, block>>>(in, out, inx, iny, inz, zChunk);\n        }\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n    }\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());\n\n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n\n    printf(\"Computation time: %ld ms\\n\", duration.count());\n\n    // Calculate performance metrics\n    double cellUpdates = (double)((nx-2) * (ny-2) * (nz-2)) * iterations;\n    double mcups = cellUpdates / (duration.count() / 1000.0) / 1e6; Boundary cells\n// are never written: they are copied unchanged by the original algorithm on\n// every iteration, so both device buffers are seeded with the initial grid and\n// their boundaries stay valid for the whole run.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nstatic constexpr int TX = 32;\nstatic constexpr int TY = 8;\n\n__global__ __launch_bounds__(TX* TY) void stencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input,\n                                                        Real* __restrict__ output,\n                                                        const int nx, const int ny, const int nz,\n                                                        const int zChunk) {\n    __shared__ Real tile[TY + 2][TX + 2];\n\n    const int tx = threadIdx.x;\n    const int ty = threadIdx.y;\n\n    const int x = 1 + blockIdx.x * TX + tx;\n    const int y = 1 + blockIdx.y * TY + ty;\n\n    // Clamped coordinates keep the (unused) shared-memory entries of threads\n    // that fall outside the grid at valid addresses.\n    const int xl = min(x, nx - 1);\n    const int yl = min(y, ny - 1);\n\n    const bool active = (x < nx - 1) && (y < ny - 1);\n\n    const size_t slice = (size_t)nx * (size_t)ny;\n    const size_t base = (size_t)yl * (size_t)nx + (size_t)xl;\n\n    const int z0 = 1 + blockIdx.z * zChunk;\n    const int z1 = min(z0 + zChunk, nz - 1);\n    if (z0 >= z1) return;\n\n    const Real* in = input + base + (size_t)z0 * slice;\n\n    Real prev = in[-(ptrdiff_t)slice];\n    Real cur = in[0];\n\n    for (int z = z0; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CehQ4h27QM2we9ta9HYRt","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01XR3EcaTVDPz81n3AbAP9XJ","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd /home/llmtest/evals/20260901-162328/stencil3d_claude-opus-5-cc-medium_cuda_r2/stencil3d && python3 - <<'PY'\np='stencil3d.cpp'\ns=open(p).read()\nstart=s.index('// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n// CUDA 7-point stencil')\nend=s.index('bool validateResult')\nnew = r'''// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Exact division by 7.\n//\n// A hardware IEEE double division costs ~25 FP64 instructions and, on the FP64-\n// throughput-limited consumer GPUs this benchmark targets, it dominates the\n// runtime of the stencil.  The Markstein refinement below (reciprocal multiply\n// + one exact FMA residual correction) yields the correctly rounded quotient -\n// bit-for-bit identical to `x / 7.0` - in three FP64 instructions.  It relies\n// on `1.0 / 7.0` being the correctly rounded reciprocal and on `fma` computing\n// the residual exactly.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n__device__ __forceinline__ Real div7(const Real x) {\n    const Real inv7 = 1.0 / 7.0;\n    const Real q = x * inv7;\n    const Real r = fma(-7.0, q, x);  // exact residual\n    return fma(r, inv7, q);\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// CUDA 7-point stencil\n//\n// Each thread owns one interior (x, y) column and marches it along z, so the\n// z-1 / z+1 neighbours stay in registers (2.5D blocking) and only one new value\n// per cell has to be fetched; the x / y neighbours are served by the caches.\n// The z range is split into chunks so that enough blocks are in flight to fill\n// the device while keeping the concurrently active blocks close together in\n// memory.\n//\n// Boundary cells are never written: the original algorithm copies them\n// unchanged on every iteration, so both device buffers are seeded with the\n// initial grid and their boundaries stay valid for the whole run.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nstatic constexpr int TX = 32;\nstatic constexpr int TY = 4;\nstatic constexpr int Z_SPLIT = 32;  // target number of z chunks\n\n__global__ __launch_bounds__(TX* TY) void stencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input,\n                                                        Real* __restrict__ output,\n                                                        const int nx, const int ny, const int nz,\n                                                        const int zChunk) {\n    const int x = 1 + blockIdx.x * TX + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = 1 + blockIdx.y * TY + threadIdx.y;\n\n    const int z0 = 1 + blockIdx.z * zChunk;\n    const int z1 = min(z0 + zChunk, nz - 1);\n    if (x >= nx - 1 || y >= ny - 1 || z0 >= z1) return;\n\n    const size_t slice = (size_t)nx * (size_t)ny;\n    const size_t base = (size_t)y * (size_t)nx + (size_t)x;\n    const Real* in = input + base + (size_t)z0 * slice;\n\n    Real prev = in[-(ptrdiff_t)slice];  // z - 1\n    Real cur = in[0];                   // z\n\n    for (int z = z0; z < z1; ++z, in += slice) {\n        const Real next = in[slice];  // z + 1\n\n        const Real center = cur;\n        const Real left = in[-1];\n        const Real right = in[1];\n        const Real front = in[-nx];\n        const Real back = in[nx];\n        const Real bottom = prev;\n        const Real top = next;\n\n        output[base + (size_t)z * slice] =\n            div7(center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top);\n\n        prev = cur;\n        cur = next;\n    }\n}\n\n'''\ns = s[:start] + new + s[end:]\n\n# launch configuration\nold_cfg_start = s.index('    dim3 block(TX, TY);')\nold_cfg_end = s.index('    // Run stencil iterations')\nnew_cfg = r'''    dim3 block(TX, TY);\n    dim3 grid(1, 1, 1);\n    int zChunk = 1;\n    if (nxi > 0 && nyi > 0 && nzi > 0) {\n        zChunk = std::max(1, nzi / Z_SPLIT);\n        grid = dim3((unsigned)((nxi + TX - 1) / TX), (unsigned)((nyi + TY - 1) / TY),\n                    (unsigned)((nzi + zChunk - 1) / zChunk));\n    }\n\n'''\ns = s[:old_cfg_start] + new_cfg + s[old_cfg_end:]\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nPY\ngrep -n \"Z_SPLIT\\|zChunk = \\|inx, iny\" stencil3d.cpp"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1918,"cache_read_input_tokens":77492,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1918},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"420ca98f-cf5c-4ea4-9f03-a9378cc7e768","uuid":"9ef1e148-4fb0-42bc-8913-09e48f85ae25","timestamp":"2026-09-03T23:47:31.017Z","request_id":"req_011CehQ4gHhwi1kzRrNNpuWU"}

Summary of the work:\n\n## What changed\n\n**`stencil3d/stencil3d.cpp`** — now a CUDA program (same file name, compiled as CUDA):\n\n- **`stencilKernel`** — 2.5D blocked 7-point stencil. Each thread owns one interior `(x,y)` column and marches along `z`, keeping the `z±1` values in registers so only one new value per cell is fetched; `x±1`/`y±1` come from cache. The z-range is split into chunks (`gridDim.z`) to expose enough blocks to fill the device while keeping concurrently-running blocks close in memory. Block `32×4`, ~`nz/32` z-chunks — measured near-optimal from 96³ to 768³.\n- **Boundary handling** — the original copies boundary cells unchanged every iteration, so both device buffers are seeded with the initial grid once and the kernel only writes the interior. Same result, no boundary pass.\n- **`div7()`** — the FP64 divide was the actual bottleneck (GA102 runs FP64 at 1/64 rate; a hardware `ddiv` is ~25 FP64 instructions). Replaced with a Markstein refinement (reciprocal multiply + exact FMA residual correction, 3 FP64 ops) that produces the **correctly rounded, bit-identical** quotient. Verified over 6.3e9 random values (full bit patterns plus realistic ranges) with zero mismatches, and on all end-to-end runs below.\n- Host side keeps the original init, timing region (kernel loop only, bracketed by `cudaDeviceSynchronize`), output, and validation; added `CUDA_CHECK` error handling.\n\n**`stencil3d/CMakeLists.txt`** — CUDA language enabled, `stencil3d.cpp` marked `LANGUAGE CUDA`, `CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES native` (overridable), same executable name and `-O3 -march=native -Wall -Wextra` for host code.\n\n## Correctness\n\nOutput is **bit-identical** to the original CPU code (full `-r` result block: sum, min/max, hash) across 10 configurations including non-multiple-of-block sizes, anisotropic grids, odd/even iteration counts, and degenerate grids (`-x 1`, `-x 2`, `3³`) where the interior is empty. `compute-sanitizer` is installed but non-functional on this machine (missing `libsanitizer-collection.so`), so no memcheck run.\n\n## Performance (RTX 3090, one GPU)\n\n| Grid | Original (1 CPU core) | CUDA |\n|---|---|---|\n| 256³ | ~429 MCUPS | **25,200 MCUPS** (~59×) |\n| 512³ | — | **27,900 MCUPS** |\n\nAt 512³ this is ~29.5 GCUPS in steady state ≈ 9 FP64 ops/cell × 29.5G = ~265 GFLOP/s, i.e. ~95% of the card's FP64 instruction issue peak — the kernel is now arithmetic-bound, not memory-bound (effective DRAM traffic ~470 GB/s of the 827 GB/s achievable).
